a,b\in A
s.t.
\forall d(d\leq a\ \&\ d\leq b\Rightarrow d\mathcal{D}c)
; then
(a\wedge b)\mathcal{D}c
, which implies, by (OR)
⊲
, that either
a\mathcal{D}c
or
b\mathcal{D}c
, as required. The proof of (ii) is similar. As to (iii), by (i), to complete the proof we need to show the ‘only if’ direction. Let
a,b,c\in A
s.t.
(a\wedge b)\mathcal{D}c
. Then, by (SI)
⊲
, the left-hand side of (UD)
⊲
is satisfied with
a_{1}:=a
,
a_{2}:=b
, and
x:=c
. Therefore,
a\mathcal{C}c
or
b\mathcal{C}c
, as required. The proof of (iv) is similar.
∎

Lemma 3.10
.

For any bounded dual proto-precontact algebra
\mathbb{D}=(A,\mathcal{D})
,

1.

If
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(SI)^{\lhd}}
, then

(i)

\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(\top)^{\lhd}}
implies
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(SF)^{\lhd}}
;

(ii)

\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(SB)^{\lhd}}
implies
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(\bot)^{\lhd}}
.

2.

If
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(WO)^{\lhd}}
, then

(i)

\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(\bot)^{\lhd}}
implies
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(SB)^{\lhd}}
;

(ii)

\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(SF)^{\lhd}}
implies
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(\top)^{\lhd}}
.

Proof.

(i) Let
a\in A
. Then by assumption,
\bot\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\top\ \&\ \bot\leq a
, which by (SI)
⊲
implies
a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\top
, as required.

(ii) (SB)
⊲
implies that
b\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bot\ \&\ b\leq\top
for some
b\in A
. Hence,
\top\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bot
follows from (SI)
⊲
, as required.
The proof of 2. is similar.
∎

3.4
Algebraizing negative and dual negative permission systems

We finish the present section by introducing the algebraic counterparts of the negative and dual negative permission systems associated with a given normative system in the literature on input/output logic. Originally introduced in the context of normative systems on classical propositional logic
[
3
]
, these notions have been generalized in
[
19
, Sections 4.1 and 4.2]
to normative systems based on arbitrary selfextensional logics. In what follows, we extend these notions to the algebraic counterparts of input/output logics on fully selfextensional logics. For any such logic
\mathcal{L}
, any
A\in\mathsf{Alg}(\mathcal{L})
, and any subset
X\subseteq A
, we let
F(X)
denote the
\mathcal{L}
-filter generated by
X
. We will write e.g.
F(a,b)
for
F(\{a,b\})
.

Definition 3.11
.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a fully selfextensional logic. For any
\mathsf{Alg}(\mathcal{L})
-based proto-subordination algebra
\mathbb{S}=(A,\prec)
, we let

\mathcal{C}_{\prec}:=\{(a,b)\mid\forall c(a\prec c\Rightarrow F(b,c)\neq A)\}\quad

\quad\quad\mathcal{D}_{\prec}:=\{(a,b)\mid\exists c(c\not\prec b\ \&\ F(a,c)=A)\}
.

The following proposition is the algebraic counterpart of
[
19
, Propositions 4.2 and 4.7]
.

Proposition 3.12
.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a selfextensional logic with
\wedge_{P}
,
\bot_{P}
,
\neg_{A}
and
\neg_{S}
. For any
\mathsf{Alg}(\mathcal{L})
-based proto-subordination algebra
\mathbb{S}=(A,\prec)
,

1.

if
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(WO)}
, then

\mathcal{C}_{\prec}=\{(a,b)\mid a\not\prec\neg b\}
;

2.

if
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(SI)}
, then
\mathcal{D}_{\prec}=\{(a,b)\mid\neg a\not\prec b\}
.

Proof.

The assumptions imply that
F(a,\neg a)=A
for every
a\in A
and
A\models\forall a\forall b(F(a,b)=A\Rightarrow b\leq\neg a)
, where
F(a,b)
is the
\mathcal{L}
-filter of
A
generated by
\{a,b\}
.

1. For the left-to-right inclusion, let
c:=\neg b
; hence
F(b,\neg b)=A
, which implies, by the definition of
\mathcal{C}_{\prec}
,
a\not\prec\neg b
, as required.
Conversely, let
a,b\in A
s.t.
a\not\prec\neg b
and let
c\in A
s.t.
F(b,c)=A
. By the preliminary observations on
A
, this implies that
c\leq\neg b
. Hence,
a\not\prec c
, for otherwise, by
\mathrm{(WO)}
,
a\prec c
and
c\leq\neg b
would imply that
a\prec\neg b
, against the assumption.

2. For the right-to-left inclusion, take
c:=\neg a
as the witness; as discussed above,
F(a,\neg a)=A
, as required.
Conversely, let
a,b\in A
s.t.
c\not\prec b
for some
c\in A
s.t.
F(a,c)=A
. As discussed above, this implies that
c\leq\neg a
. Hence,
\neg a\not\prec b
, for otherwise, by
\mathrm{(SI)}
,
c\prec b
, against the assumption.
∎

Definition 3.13
.

For any proto-subordination algebra
\mathbb{S}=(A,\prec)
,

1.

the proto-precontact algebra associated with
\mathbb{S}
is
\mathbb{S}_{{\rhd}}:=(A,\mathcal{C}_{\prec})
;

2.

the dual proto-precontact algebra associated with
\mathbb{S}
is
\mathbb{S}_{{\lhd}}:=(A,\mathcal{D}_{\prec})
.

The following is a straightforward consequence of Proposition
3.12
.

Corollary 3.14
.

Let
\mathbb{S}=(A,\prec)
be a proto-subordination algebra as in Proposition
3.12
. For any
\mathrm{(X)\ \in\{(\top),(\bot),}

\mathrm{(SB),(SF),(SI),(WO),(DD),(UD),(AND),(OR)}\}
,

1.

\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(X)}\,
iff
\,\mathbb{S}_{\rhd}\models\mathrm{(X)^{{\rhd}}}
.

2.

\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(X)}\,
iff
\,\mathbb{S}_{\lhd}\models\mathrm{(X)}^{\lhd}
.

The corollary above can be further refined to various more general classes of proto-subordination algebras, so as to extend
[
19
, Propositions 4.5 and 4.8]
to an algebraic setting.

An
\mathsf{Alg}(\mathcal{L})
proto-subordination algebra
\mathbb{S}=(A,\prec)
is
internally incoherent
if
a\prec b
and
a\prec c
for some
a,b,c\in A
such that
F(a)\neq A
and
F(b,c)=A
;
\mathbb{S}
is
internally coherent
if it is not internally incoherent.

4
Slanted algebras arising from defined relational algebras

Definition 4.1
.

Let
\mathbb{S}=(A,\prec)
,
\mathbb{C}=(A,\mathcal{C})
, and
\mathbb{D}=(A,\mathcal{D})
be a proto-subordination algebra, a proto-precontact algebra, and a dual proto-precontact algebra, respectively.

1.

if
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(DD)+(UD)+(SB)+(SF)}
, then the slanted algebra associated with
\mathbb{S}
is
\mathbb{S}^{*}=(A,\Diamond,\blacksquare)
s.t.
\Diamond a\coloneqq\bigwedge{\prec}[a]
and
\blacksquare a\coloneqq\bigvee{\prec^{-1}}[a]
for any
a
.

2.

if
\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(DD)^{\rhd}+(UD)^{\rhd}+(SB)^{\rhd}+(SF)^{\rhd}}
, then the slanted algebra associated with
\mathbb{C}
is
\mathbb{C}^{*}=(A,{\rhd},{\blacktriangleright})
s.t.
{\rhd}a\coloneqq\bigvee(\mathcal{C}[a])^{c}
and
{\blacktriangleright}a\coloneqq\bigvee({\mathcal{C}^{-1}}[a])^{c}
for any
a
.

3.

\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(DD)^{\lhd}+(UD)^{\lhd}+(SB)^{\lhd}+(SF)^{\lhd}}
, then the slanted algebra associated with
\mathbb{D}
is
\mathbb{D}^{*}=(A,{\lhd},{\blacktriangleleft})
s.t.
{\lhd}a\coloneqq\bigwedge(\mathcal{D}[a])^{c}
and
{\blacktriangleleft}a\coloneqq\bigwedge({\mathcal{D}^{-1}}[a])^{c}
for any
a
.

The assumption
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(DD)}
implies that
{\prec}[a]
is down-directed for every
a\in A
, and
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(SF)}
implies that
{\prec}[a]\neq\varnothing
. Hence,
\Diamond a\in K(A^{\delta})
. Likewise,
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(UD)+(SB)}
guarantees that
\blacksquare a\in O(A^{\delta})
, and
\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(DD)^{\rhd}+(SF)^{\rhd}}
(resp.
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(DD)^{\lhd}+(SF)^{\lhd}}
) guarantees that
{\rhd}a\in O(A^{\delta})
(resp.
{\lhd}a\in K(A^{\delta})
) and
\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(UD)^{\rhd}+(SB)^{\rhd}}
(resp.
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(UD)^{\lhd}+(SB)^{\lhd}}
) guarantees that
{\blacktriangleright}a\in O(A^{\delta})
(resp.
{\blacktriangleleft}a\in K(A^{\delta})
) for all
a\in A
.

Lemma 4.2
.

For any proto-subordination algebra
\mathbb{S}=(A,\prec)
, any proto-precontact algebra
\mathbb{C}=(A,\mathcal{C})
, any dual proto-precontact algebra
\mathbb{D}=(A,\mathcal{D})
and all
a,b\in A
,

1.

if
\mathbb{S}
is defined (i.e.
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(DD)+(SF)+(UD)+(SB)}
), then

(i)

a\prec b
implies
\Diamond a\leq b
and
a\leq\blacksquare b
.

(ii)

if
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(WO)}
, then
\Diamond a\leq b
iff
a\prec b
.

(iii)

if
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(SI)}
, then
a\leq\blacksquare b
iff
a\prec b
.

2.

if
\mathbb{C}
is defined (i.e.
\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(DD)^{\rhd}+(SF)^{\rhd}+(UD)^{\rhd}+(SB)^{\rhd}}
), then

(i)

a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}b
implies
b\leq{\rhd}a
and
a\leq{\blacktriangleright}b
.

(ii)

if
\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(WO)^{\rhd}}
, then
b\leq{\rhd}a
iff
a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}b
.

(iii)

if
\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(SI)^{\rhd}}
, then
a\leq{\blacktriangleright}b
iff
a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}b
.

3.

if
\mathbb{D}
is defined (i.e.
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(DD)^{\lhd}+(SF)^{\lhd}+(UD)^{\lhd}+(SB)^{\lhd}}
), then

(i)

a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}b
implies
{\lhd}a\leq b
and
{\blacktriangleleft}b\leq a
.

(ii)

if
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(WO)^{\lhd}}
, then
{\lhd}a\leq b
iff
a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}b
.

(iii)

if
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(SI)^{\lhd}}
, then
{\blacktriangleleft}b\leq a
iff
a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}b
.

Proof.

Below, we group together the proofs of sub-items (i) of each item, and likewise for sub-items (ii) and (iii).

(i)
a\prec b
iff
b\in{\prec}[a]
iff
a\in{\prec}^{-1}[b]
, hence
a\prec b
implies
b\geq\bigwedge{\prec}[a]=\Diamond a
and
a\leq\bigvee{\prec}^{-1}[b]=\blacksquare b
.

Likewise,
a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}b
iff
b\notin{\mathcal{C}}[a]
(i.e.
b\in({\mathcal{C}}[a])^{c}
) iff
a\notin{\mathcal{C}}^{-1}[b]
(i.e.
a\in({\mathcal{C}}^{-1}[b])^{c}
). Hence,
a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}b
implies
b\leq\bigvee({\mathcal{C}}[a])^{c}={\rhd}a
and
a\leq\bigvee({\mathcal{C}}^{-1}[b])^{c}={\blacktriangleright}b
. Finally,
a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}b
iff
b\notin{\mathcal{D}}[a]
(i.e.
b\in({\mathcal{D}}[a])^{c}
) iff
a\notin{\mathcal{D}}^{-1}[b]
(i.e.
a\in({\mathcal{D}}^{-1}[b])^{c}
). Hence,
a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}b
implies
b\geq\bigwedge({\mathcal{D}}[a])^{c}={\lhd}a
and
a\geq\bigwedge({\mathcal{D}}^{-1}[b])^{c}={\blacktriangleleft}b
.

(ii) By (i), to complete the proof, we need to show the ‘only if’ direction. The assumption
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(DD)+(SF)}
implies that
{\prec}[a]
is non-empty and down-directed for any
a\in A
. Hence, by compactness,
\bigwedge{\prec}[a]=\Diamond a\leq b
implies that
c\leq b
for some
c\in{\prec}[a]
, i.e.
a\prec c\leq b
for some
c\in A
, and by (WO), this implies that
a\prec b
, as required.

Likewise, the assumption
\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(DD)^{\rhd}+(SF)^{\rhd}}
implies that
({\mathcal{C}}[a])^{c}
is non-empty and up-directed for any
a\in A
. Hence, by compactness,
b\leq{\rhd}a=\bigvee({\mathcal{C}}[a])^{c}
implies that
b\leq c
for some
c\in({\mathcal{C}}[a])^{c}
(i.e.
a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}c
), i.e.
a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}c
and
b\leq c
for some
c\in A
, i.e.
a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}c
and
b\geq c
for some
c\in A
. By
\mathrm{(WO)}^{\rhd}
, this implies that
a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}b
, as required.

Finally,
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(DD)^{\lhd}+(SF)^{\lhd}}
implies that
({\mathcal{D}}[a])^{c}
is non-empty and down-directed for any
a\in A
. Hence, by compactness,
\bigwedge({\mathcal{D}}[a])^{c}={\lhd}a\leq b
implies that
c\leq b
for some
c\in({\mathcal{D}}[a])^{c}
(i.e.
a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}c
), i.e.
a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}c
and
c\leq b
for some
c\in A
. By
\mathrm{(WO)}^{\lhd}
, this implies that
a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}b
, as required.

(iii) is proven similarly to (ii), by observing that
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(UD)+(SB)}
implies that
{\prec}^{-1}[a]
is non-empty and up-directed for every
a\in A
, that
\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(UD)^{\rhd}+(SB)^{\rhd}}
implies that
(\mathcal{C}^{-1}[a])^{c}
is nonempty and up-directed for every
a\in A
, and that
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(UD)^{\lhd}+(SB)^{\lhd}}
implies that
({\mathcal{D}}^{-1}[a])^{c}
is non-empty and down-directed for every
a\in A
.
∎

An immediate consequence of the lemma above and Corollary
2.11
is the following

Corollary 4.3
.

For any defined proto-subordination algebra
\mathbb{S}=(A,\prec)
, any defined proto-precontact algebra
\mathbb{C}=(A,\mathcal{C})
, and any defined dual proto-precontact algebra
\mathbb{D}=(A,\mathcal{D})
, if
A
is a bounded lattice, then

1.

if
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(SI)+(WO)}
, then
\mathbb{S}^{\ast}
is tense, hence is normal.

2.

if
\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(SI)^{\rhd}+(WO)^{\rhd}}
, then
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
is tense, hence is normal.

3.

if
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(SI)^{\lhd}+(WO)^{\lhd}}
, then
\mathbb{D}^{\ast}
is tense, hence is normal.

Lemma 4.4
.

For any defined proto-subordination algebra
\mathbb{S}=(A,\prec)
, any defined proto-precontact algebra
\mathbb{C}=(A,\mathcal{C})
, and any defined dual proto-precontact algebra
\mathbb{D}=(A,\mathcal{D})
,

1.

If
\mathbb{S}
is bounded and
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(\bot)}
, then
\mathbb{S}^{\ast}\models\Diamond\bot\leq\bot
.

2.

If
\mathbb{S}
is bounded and
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(\top)}
, then
\mathbb{S}^{\ast}\models\top\leq\blacksquare\top
.

3.

If
\mathbb{C}
is bounded and
\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(\bot)^{\rhd}}
, then
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}\models\top\leq{\rhd}\bot
.

4.

If
\mathbb{C}
is bounded and
\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(\top)^{\rhd}}
, then
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}\models\top\leq{\blacktriangleright}\bot
.

5.

If
\mathbb{D}
is bounded and
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(\bot)^{\lhd}}
, then
\mathbb{D}^{\ast}\models{\lhd}\top\leq\bot
.

6.

If
\mathbb{D}
is bounded and
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(\top)^{\lhd}}
, then
\mathbb{D}^{\ast}\models{\blacktriangleleft}\top\leq\bot
.

7.

If
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(SI)}
, then

\Diamond
on
\mathbb{S}^{*}
is monotone;

8.

if
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(WO)}
, then

\blacksquare
on
\mathbb{S}^{*}
is monotone.

9.

If
\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(SI)^{\rhd}}
, then

{\rhd}
on
\mathbb{C}^{*}
is antitone.

10.

If
\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(WO)^{\rhd}}
, then

{\blacktriangleright}
on
\mathbb{C}^{*}
is antitone.

11.

If
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(SI)^{\lhd}}
, then
{\lhd}
on
\mathbb{D}^{*}
is antitone.

12.

If
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(WO)^{\lhd}}
, then

{\blacktriangleleft}
on
\mathbb{D}^{*}
is antitone.

Proof.

1. By assumption,
\bot\in{\prec}[\bot]
; hence,
\Diamond\bot=\bigwedge{\prec}[\bot]\leq\bot
, as required. The proofs of 2-6 are similar.
7. Let
a,b\in A
s.t.
a\leq b
. To show that
\Diamond a=\bigwedge{\prec}[a]\leq\bigwedge{\prec}[b]=\Diamond b
, it is enough to show that
{\prec}[b]\subseteq{\prec}[a]
, i.e. that if
x\in A
and
b\prec x
, then
a\prec x
. Indeed, by (SI),
a\leq b\prec x
implies
a\prec x
, as required. Item 8 is shown similarly. Likewise, as to 9, to show that
{\rhd}b=\bigvee({\mathcal{C}}[b])^{c}\leq\bigvee({\mathcal{C}}[a])^{c}={\rhd}a
whenever
a\leq b
, it is enough to show that
({\mathcal{C}}[b])^{c}\subseteq({\mathcal{C}}[a])^{c}
, i.e. that
{\mathcal{C}}[a]\subseteq{\mathcal{C}}[b]
, i.e. that if
c\in A
and
a\mathcal{C}c
, then
b\mathcal{C}c
, which is exactly what is guaranteed by (SI)
⊳
under
a\leq b
. The proofs of 10-12 are similar.
∎

The following lemma shows that the converse conditions of Lemma
4.4
hold under additional assumptions.

Lemma 4.5
.

For any defined proto-subordination algebra
\mathbb{S}=(A,\prec)
, any defined proto-precontact algebra
\mathbb{C}=(A,\mathcal{C})
, and any defined dual proto-precontact algebra
\mathbb{D}=(A,\mathcal{D})
,

1.

If
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(WO)}
, then

(i)

\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(SI)}\quad
iff
\quad\Diamond
on
\mathbb{S}^{\ast}
is monotone.

(ii)

if
\mathbb{S}
is
\vee
-SL based, then
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(OR)}\quad
iff
\quad\mathbb{S}^{\ast}\models\Diamond(a\vee b)\leq\Diamond a\vee\Diamond b
.

(iii)

if
\mathbb{S}
is bounded, then
\mathbb{S}\models(\bot)\quad
iff
\quad\mathbb{S}^{\ast}\models\Diamond\bot\leq\bot
.

2.

If
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(SI)}
, then

(i)

\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(WO)}\quad
iff
\quad\blacksquare
on
\mathbb{S}^{\ast}
is monotone.

(ii)

if
\mathbb{S}
is
\wedge
-SL based, then
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(AND)}\quad
iff
\quad\mathbb{S}^{\ast}\models\blacksquare a\wedge\blacksquare b\leq\blacksquare(a\wedge b)
.

(iii)

if
\mathbb{S}
is bounded, then
\mathbb{S}\models(\top)\quad
iff
\quad\mathbb{S}^{\ast}\models\top\leq\blacksquare\top
.

3.

If
\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(WO)^{\rhd}}
, then

(i)

\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(SI)^{\rhd}}\quad
iff
\quad{\rhd}
on
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
is antitone.

(ii)

if
\mathbb{C}
is
\vee
-SL based, then
\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(OR)^{\rhd}}\quad
iff
\quad\mathbb{C}^{\ast}\models{\rhd}a\wedge{\rhd}b\leq{\rhd}(a\vee b)
.

(iii)

if
\mathbb{C}
is bounded, then
\mathbb{C}\models(\bot)^{\rhd}\quad
iff
\quad\mathbb{C}^{\ast}\models\top\leq{\rhd}\bot
.

4.

If
\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(SI)^{\rhd}}
, then

(i)

\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(WO)^{\rhd}}\quad
iff
\quad{\blacktriangleright}
on
\mathbb{C}^{\ast}
is antitone.

(ii)

if
\mathbb{C}
is
\vee
-SL based, then
\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(AND)^{\rhd}}\;
iff
\;\mathbb{C}^{\ast}\models{\blacktriangleright}a\wedge{\blacktriangleright}b\leq{\blacktriangleright}(a\vee b)
.

(iii)

if
\mathbb{C}
is bounded, then
\mathbb{C}\models(\top)^{\rhd}\quad
iff
\quad\mathbb{C}^{\ast}\models\top\leq{\blacktriangleright}\bot
.

5.

If
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(WO)^{\lhd}}
, then

(i)

\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(SI)^{\lhd}}\quad
iff
\quad{\lhd}
on
\mathbb{D}^{\ast}
is antitone.

(ii)

if
\mathbb{D}
is
\vee
-SL based, then
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(OR)^{\lhd}}\quad
iff
\quad\mathbb{D}^{\ast}\models{\lhd}(a\wedge b)\leq{\lhd}a\vee{\lhd}b
.

(iii)

if
\mathbb{D}
is bounded, then
\mathbb{D}\models(\bot)^{\lhd}\quad
iff
\quad\mathbb{D}^{\ast}\models{\lhd}\top\leq\bot
.

6.

If
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(SI)^{\lhd}}
, then

(i)

\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(WO)^{\lhd}}\quad
iff
\quad{\blacktriangleleft}
on
\mathbb{D}^{\ast}
is antitone.

(ii)

if
\mathbb{D}
is
\vee
-SL based, then
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(AND)^{\lhd}}\;
iff
\;\mathbb{D}^{\ast}\models{\blacktriangleleft}(a\wedge b)\leq{\blacktriangleleft}a\vee{\blacktriangleleft}b
.

(iii)

if
\mathbb{D}
is bounded, then
\mathbb{D}\models(\top)^{\lhd}\quad
iff
\quad\mathbb{D}^{\ast}\models{\blacktriangleleft}\top\leq\bot
.

Proof.

1. (i) By Lemma
4.4
7, the proof is complete if we show the ‘if’ direction. Let
a,b,x\in A
s.t.
a\leq b\prec x
. By Lemma
4.2
1(ii), to show that
a\prec x
, it is enough to show that
\Diamond a\leq x
. Since
\Diamond
is monotone,
a\leq b
implies
\Diamond a\leq\Diamond b
, and, by Lemma
4.2
1(i),
b\prec x
implies that
\Diamond b\leq x
. Hence,
\Diamond a\leq x
, as required.

(ii) From left to right, let
a,b\in A
. Since both
\Diamond(a\vee b)
and
\Diamond a\vee\Diamond b
are closed elements of
A^{\delta}
(cf. Proposition
2.6
1(iv)), by Proposition
2.6
1(i), to show that
\Diamond(a\vee b)\leq\Diamond a\vee\Diamond b
, it is enough to show that for any
x\in A
, if
\Diamond a\vee\Diamond b\leq x
, then
\Diamond(a\vee b)\leq x
.

\Diamond a\vee\Diamond b\leq x

iff

\Diamond a\leq x
and
\Diamond b\leq x

iff

a\prec x
and
b\prec x

Lemma
4.2
.1(ii) (WO)

implies

a\vee b\prec x

(OR)

implies

\Diamond(a\vee b)\leq x

Lemma
4.2
.1(i)

Conversely, let
a,b,x\in A
s.t.
a\prec x
and
b\prec x
. By Lemma
4.2
(ii), to show that
a\vee b\prec x
, it is enough to show that
\Diamond(a\vee b)\leq x
, and since
\mathbb{S}^{\ast}\models\Diamond(a\vee b)\leq\Diamond a\vee\Diamond b
, it is enough to show that
\Diamond a\vee\Diamond b\leq x
, i.e. that
\Diamond a\leq x
and
\Diamond b\leq x
. These two inequalities hold by Lemma
4.2
1(i), and the assumptions on
a,b
and
x
.

(iii) By Lemma
4.2
(ii),
\bot\prec\bot
is equivalent to
\Diamond\bot\leq\bot
, as required.
2. is proven similarly.

3. (i) By Lemma
4.4
9, the proof is complete if we show the ‘if’ direction. Let
a,b,x\in A
s.t.
a\leq b\cancel{\mathcal{C}}x
. By Lemma
4.2
2(ii), to show that
a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}x
, it is enough to show that
x\leq{\rhd}a
. Since
{\rhd}
is antitone,
a\leq b
implies
{\rhd}b\leq{\rhd}a
, and, by Lemma
4.2
2(i),
b\cancel{\mathcal{C}}x
implies that
x\leq{\rhd}b
. Hence,
x\leq{\rhd}a
, as required.

(ii) From left to right, let
a,b\in A
. Since both
{\rhd}(a\vee b)
and
{\rhd}a\wedge{\rhd}b
are open elements of
A^{\delta}
(cf. Proposition
2.6
1(v)), by Proposition
2.6
1(ii), to show that
{\rhd}a\wedge{\rhd}b\leq{\rhd}(a\vee b)
, it is enough to show that for any
x\in A
, if
x\leq{\rhd}a\wedge{\rhd}b
, then
x\leq{\rhd}(a\vee b)
.

x\leq{\rhd}a\wedge{\rhd}b

iff

x\leq{\rhd}a
and
x\leq{\rhd}b

iff

x\cancel{\mathcal{C}}a
and
x\cancel{\mathcal{C}}b

Lemma
4.2
.2(ii) (WO)
⊳

implies

(a\vee b)\cancel{\mathcal{C}}x

(OR)
⊳

implies

x\leq{\rhd}(a\vee b)

Lemma
4.2
.2(i)

Conversely, let
a,b,x\in A
s.t.
a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}x
and
b\cancel{\mathcal{C}}x
. By Lemma
4.2
.2(ii), to show that
(a\vee b)\cancel{\mathcal{C}}x
, it is enough to show that
x\leq{\rhd}(a\vee b)
, and since
\mathbb{S}^{\ast}\models{\rhd}a\wedge{\rhd}b\leq{\rhd}(a\vee b)
, it is enough to show that
x\leq{\rhd}a\wedge{\rhd}b
, i.e. that
x\leq{\rhd}a
and
x\leq{\rhd}b
. These two inequalities hold by Lemma
4.2
.2(i), and the assumptions on
a,b
and
x
.

(iii) By Lemma
4.2
.2(ii),
\bot\cancel{\mathcal{C}}\top
is equivalent to
\top\leq{\rhd}\bot
, as required.
4. is proven similarly.

5. (i) By Lemma
4.4
.11, the proof is complete if we show the ‘if’ direction. For contraposition, let
a,b,x\in A
s.t.
a\leq b\ \&\ a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}x
. By Lemma
4.2
.3(ii), to show that
b\cancel{\mathcal{D}}x
, it is enough to show that
{\lhd}b\leq x
. Since
{\lhd}
is antitone,
a\leq b
implies
{\lhd}b\leq{\lhd}a
, and, by Lemma
4.2
.3(i),
a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}x
implies that
{\lhd}a\leq x
. Hence,
{\lhd}b\leq x
, as required.

(ii) From left to right, let
a,b\in A
. Since both
{\lhd}(a\wedge b)
and
{\lhd}a\vee{\lhd}b
are closed elements of
A^{\delta}
(cf. Proposition
2.6
.1(iv)), by Proposition
2.6
.1(i), to show that
{\lhd}(a\wedge b)\leq{\lhd}a\vee{\lhd}b
, it is enough to show that for any
x\in A
, if
{\lhd}a\vee{\lhd}b\leq x
, then
{\lhd}(a\wedge b)\leq x
.

{\lhd}a\vee{\lhd}b\leq x

iff

{\lhd}a\leq x\ \&\ {\lhd}b\leq x

iff

a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}x
and
b\cancel{\mathcal{D}}b

Lemma
4.2
.3(ii) (WO)
⊲

implies

(a\wedge b)\cancel{\mathcal{D}}x

(OR)
⊲

implies

{\lhd}(a\wedge b)\leq x

Lemma
4.2
.3(i)

Conversely, for contraposition, let
a,b,x\in A
s.t.
a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}x
and
b\cancel{\mathcal{D}}x
. By Lemma
4.2
.3(ii), to show that
(a\wedge b)\cancel{\mathcal{D}}x
, it is enough to show that
{\lhd}(a\wedge b)\leq x
, and since
\mathbb{S}^{\ast}\models{\lhd}(a\wedge b)\leq{\lhd}a\vee{\lhd}b
, it is enough to show that
{\lhd}a\vee{\lhd}b\leq x
, i.e. that
{\lhd}a\leq x
and
{\lhd}b\leq x
. These two inequalities hold by Lemma
4.2
.3(i), and the assumptions on
a,b
and
x
.

(iii) By Lemma
4.2
.3(ii),
\top\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bot
is equivalent to
{\lhd}\top\leq\bot
, as required.
6. is proven similarly. ∎

Corollary 4.6
.

For any proto-subordination algebra
\mathbb{S}=(A,\prec)
, any proto-precontact algebra
\mathbb{C}=(A,\mathcal{C})
, and any dual proto-precontact algebra
\mathbb{D}=(A,\mathcal{D})
,

1.

if
\mathbb{S}
is defined, then

(i)

\mathbb{S}
is monotone iff
\mathbb{S}^{*}
is monotone;

(ii)

\mathbb{S}
is regular iff
\mathbb{S}^{*}
is regular;

(iii)

\mathbb{S}
is a subordination algebra iff
\mathbb{S}^{*}
is normal.

2.

if
\mathbb{C}
is defined, then

(i)

\mathbb{C}
is antitone iff
\mathbb{C}^{*}
is antitone;

(ii)

\mathbb{C}
is regular iff
\mathbb{C}^{*}
is regular;

(iii)

\mathbb{C}
is a precontact algebra iff
\mathbb{C}^{*}
is normal.

3.

if
\mathbb{D}
is defined, then

(i)

\mathbb{D}
is antitone iff
\mathbb{D}^{*}
is antitone;

(ii)

\mathbb{D}
is regular iff
\mathbb{D}^{*}
is regular;

(iii)

\mathbb{D}
is a dual precontact algebra iff
\mathbb{D}^{*}
is normal.

Lemma 4.7
.

For any proto-subordination algebra
\mathbb{S}=(A,\prec)
, any proto-precontact algebra
\mathbb{C}=(A,\mathcal{C})
and any dual proto-precontact algebra
\mathbb{D}=(A,\mathcal{D})
, for all
a,b\in A
,
k,h\in K(A^{\delta})
, and
o,p\in O(A^{\delta})
, and all
D\subseteq A
nonempty and down-directed, and
U\subseteq A
nonempty and up-directed,

1.

if
\mathbb{S}
is
\Diamond
-monotone (i.e.
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(SF)+(DD)+(WO)+(SI)}
), then

(i)

{\prec}[D]\coloneqq\{c\ |\ \exists a(a\in D\ \&\ a\prec c)\}
is nonempty and down-directed;

(ii)

if
k=\bigwedge D
, then
\Diamond k=\bigwedge{\prec}[D]\in K(A^{\delta})
;

(iii)

\Diamond k\leq b
implies
a\prec b
for some
a\in A
s.t.
k\leq a
;

(iv)

\Diamond k\leq o
implies
a\prec b
for some
a,b\in A
s.t.
k\leq a
and
b\leq o
.

2.

if
\mathbb{S}
is
\blacksquare
-monotone (i.e.
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(SB)+(UD)+(WO)+(SI)}
), then

(i)

{\prec}^{-1}[U]\coloneqq\{c\ |\ \exists a(a\in U\ \&\ c\prec a)\}
is nonempty and up-directed;

(ii)

if
o=\bigvee U
, then
\blacksquare o=\bigvee{\prec}^{-1}[U]\in O(A^{\delta})
;

(iii)

a\leq\blacksquare o
implies
a\prec b
for some
b\in A
s.t.
b\leq o
.

(iv)

k\leq\blacksquare o
implies
a\prec b
for some
a,b\in A
s.t.
k\leq a
and
b\leq o
.

3.

if
\mathbb{C}
is
{\rhd}
-antitone (i.e.
\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(SF)^{\rhd}+(DD)^{\rhd}+(WO)^{\rhd}+(SI)^{\rhd}}
), then

(i)

{\cancel{\mathcal{C}}}[D]\coloneqq\{c\ |\ \exists a(a\in D\ \&\ a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}c)\}
is nonempty and up-directed;

(ii)

if
k=\bigwedge D
, then
{\rhd}k=\bigvee{\cancel{\mathcal{C}}}[D]\in O(A^{\delta})
;

(iii)

a\leq{\rhd}k
implies
c\cancel{\mathcal{C}}a
for some
c\in A
s.t.
k\leq c
;

(iv)

h\leq{\rhd}k
implies
c\cancel{\mathcal{C}}b
for some
b,c\in A
s.t.
h\leq b
and
k\leq c
.

4.

if
\mathbb{C}
is
{\blacktriangleright}
-antitone (i.e.
\mathbb{C}\models\mathrm{(SB)^{\rhd}+(UD)^{\rhd}+(WO)^{\rhd}+(SI)^{\rhd}}
), then

(i)

{\cancel{\mathcal{C}}}^{-1}[D]\coloneqq\{c\ |\ \exists a(a\in D\ \&\ c\cancel{\mathcal{C}}a)\}
is nonempty and up-directed;

(ii)

if
k=\bigwedge D
, then
{\blacktriangleright}k=\bigvee{\cancel{\mathcal{C}}}^{-1}[D]\in O(A^{\delta})
;

(iii)

a\leq{\blacktriangleright}k
implies
a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}c
for some
c\in A
s.t.
k\leq c
;

(iv)

h\leq{\blacktriangleright}k
implies
b\cancel{\mathcal{C}}c
for some
b,c\in A
s.t.
h\leq b
and
k\leq c
.

5.

if
\mathbb{D}
is
{\lhd}
-antitone (i.e.
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(SF)^{\lhd}+(DD)^{\lhd}+(WO)^{\lhd}+(SI)^{\lhd}}
), then

(i)

\cancel{\mathcal{D}}[U]\coloneqq\{c\ |\ \exists a(a\in U\ \&\ a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}c)\}
is nonempty and down-directed;

(ii)

if
o=\bigvee U
, then
{\lhd}o=\bigwedge\cancel{\mathcal{D}}[U]\in K(A^{\delta})
;

(iii)

{\lhd}o\leq a
implies
c\cancel{\mathcal{D}}a
for some
c\in A
s.t.
c\leq o
;

(iv)

{\lhd}o\leq p
implies
c\cancel{\mathcal{D}}a
for some
a,c\in A
s.t.
c\leq o
and
a\leq p
.

6.

if
\mathbb{D}
is
{\blacktriangleleft}
-antitone (i.e.
\mathbb{D}\models\mathrm{(SB)^{\lhd}+(UD)^{\lhd}+(WO)^{\lhd}+(SI)^{\lhd}}
), then

(i)

{\cancel{\mathcal{C}}}^{-1}[U]\coloneqq\{c\ |\ \exists a(a\in U\ \&\ c\cancel{\mathcal{D}}a)\}
is nonempty and down-directed;

(ii)

if
o=\bigvee U
, then
{\blacktriangleleft}o=\bigwedge{\cancel{\mathcal{D}}}^{-1}[U]\in K(A^{\delta})
;

(iii)

{\blacktriangleleft}o\leq a
implies
a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}c
for some
c\in A
s.t.
c\leq o
;

(iv)

{\blacktriangleleft}o\leq p
implies
a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}c
for some
a,c\in A
s.t.
a\leq p
and
c\leq o
.

Proof.

1. (i) By (SF),
D
nonempty implies that so is
{\prec}[D]
. If
c_{i}\in{\prec}[D]
for
1\leq i\leq 2
, then
a_{i}\prec c_{i}
for some
a_{i}\in D
. Since
D
is down-directed, some
a\in D
exists s.t.
a\leq a_{i}
for each
i
. Thus,
\mathrm{(SI)}
implies that
a\prec c_{i}
, from which the claim follows by
\mathrm{(DD)}
.

(ii) By definition,
\Diamond k\coloneqq\bigwedge\{\Diamond a\ |\ a\in A,k\leq a\}=\bigwedge\{c\ |\ \exists a(a\prec c\ \&\ k\leq a)\}
. Since
k=\bigwedge D
, by compactness,
k\leq a
iff
d\leq a
for some
d\in D
. Thus,
\Diamond k=\bigwedge\{c\ |\ \exists a(a\prec c\ \&\ k\leq a)\}=\bigwedge\{c\ |\ \exists a(a\in\lfloor D\rfloor\ \&\ a\prec c)\}=\bigwedge{\prec}[D]\in K(A^{\delta})
, the last membership holding by (i).

(iii) Let
k=\bigwedge D
for some
D\subseteq A
nonempty and down-directed. By (ii),
K(A^{\delta})\ni\Diamond k=\bigwedge{\prec}[D]
. Hence,
\Diamond k\leq b
implies by compactness that
c\leq b
for some
c\in A
s.t.
a\prec c
for some
a\in D
(hence
k=\bigwedge D\leq a
). By
\mathrm{(WO)}
, this implies that
a\prec b
for some
a\in A
s.t.
k\leq a
, as required.

(iv) By (ii),
\Diamond k\in K(A^{\delta})
. Moreover,
o\in O(A^{\delta})
implies that
o=\bigvee U
for some nonempty and up-directed
U\subseteq A
. Hence, by compactness and (iii),
\Diamond k\leq o
implies that
a\prec b
for some
a\in A
s.t.
k\leq a
and some
b\in U
(for which
b\leq o
). The proof of 2 is similar.

3. (i) By (SF)
⊳
,
D
is nonempty implies that so is
\cancel{\mathcal{C}}[D]
. If
c_{i}\in\cancel{\mathcal{C}}[D]
for
1\leq i\leq 2
, then
a_{i}\cancel{\mathcal{C}}c_{i}
for some
a_{i}\in D
. Since
D
is down-directed, some
a\in D
exists s.t.
a\leq a_{i}
for each
i
. Thus,
\mathrm{(SI)^{\rhd}}
implies that
a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}c_{i}
, from which the claim follows by
\mathrm{(DD)^{\rhd}}
.

(ii) By definition,
{\rhd}k\coloneqq\bigvee\{{\rhd}a\ |\ a\in A,k\leq a\}=\bigvee\{c\ |\ \exists a(a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}c\ \&\ k\leq a)\}
. Since
k=\bigwedge D
, by compactness,
k\leq a
iff
d\leq a
for some
d\in D
. Thus,
{\rhd}k=\bigvee\{c\ |\ \exists a(a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}c\ \&\ k\leq a)\}=\bigvee\{c\ |\ \exists a(a\in\lfloor D\rfloor\ \&\ a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}c)\}=\bigvee\cancel{\mathcal{C}}[D]\in O(A^{\delta})
, the last membership holding by (i).

(iii) Let
k=\bigwedge D
for some
D\subseteq A
nonempty and down-directed. By (ii),
O(A^{\delta})\ni{\rhd}k=\bigvee\cancel{\mathcal{C}}[D]
. Hence,
a\leq{\rhd}k
implies by compactness that
a\leq b
for some
b\in A
s.t.
c\cancel{\mathcal{C}}b
for some
c\in D
(hence
k=\bigwedge D\leq c
). By
\mathrm{(WO)}^{\rhd}
, this implies that
c\cancel{\mathcal{C}}a
for some
c\in A
s.t.
k\leq c
, as required.

(iv) By (ii),
{\rhd}k\in O(A^{\delta})
. Moreover,
h\in K(A^{\delta})
implies that
h=\bigwedge E
for some nonempty and down-directed
E\subseteq A
. Hence, by compactness and (iii),
h\leq{\rhd}k
implies that
h\leq b
for some
b\in\cancel{\mathcal{C}}[D]
, i.e.
c\cancel{\mathcal{C}}b
for some
c\in D
(for which
k\leq c
). The proof of 4 is similar.

5. (i) By (SF)
⊲
,
D
is nonempty implies that so is
\cancel{\mathcal{D}}[U]
. If
c_{i}\in\cancel{\mathcal{D}}[U]
for
1\leq i\leq 2
, then
a_{i}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}c_{i}
for some
a_{i}\in U
. Since
U
is up-directed, some
a\in U
exists s.t.
a_{i}\leq a
for each
i
. Thus,
\mathrm{(SI)^{\lhd}}
implies that
a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}c_{i}
, from which the claim follows by
\mathrm{(DD)^{\lhd}}
.

(ii) By definition,
{\lhd}o\coloneqq\bigwedge\{{\lhd}a\ |\ a\in A,a\leq o\}=\bigwedge\{c\ |\ \exists a(a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}c\ \&\ a\leq o)\}
. Since
o=\bigvee U
, by compactness,
a\leq o
iff
a\leq d
for some
d\in U
. Thus,
{\lhd}o=\bigwedge\{c\ |\ \exists a(a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}c\ \&\ a\leq o)\}=\bigwedge\{c\ |\ \exists a(a\in\lceil U\rceil\ \&\ a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}c)\}=\bigwedge\cancel{\mathcal{D}}[U]\in K(A^{\delta})
, the last membership holding by (i).

(iii) Let
o=\bigvee U
for some
U\subseteq A
nonempty and down-directed. By (ii),
K(A^{\delta})\ni{\lhd}o=\bigwedge\cancel{\mathcal{D}}[U]
. Hence,
{\lhd}o\leq a
implies by compactness that
b\leq a
for some
b\in A
s.t.
c\cancel{\mathcal{D}}b
for some
c\in U
(hence
o=\bigvee U\geq c
). By
\mathrm{(WO)}^{\lhd}
, this implies that
c\cancel{\mathcal{D}}a
for some
c\in A
s.t.
c\leq o
, as required.

(iv) By (ii),
{\lhd}o\in K(A^{\delta})
. Moreover,
p\in O(A^{\delta})
implies that
p=\bigvee V
for some nonempty and up-directed
V\subseteq A
. Hence, by compactness and (iii),
{\lhd}o\leq p
implies that
b\leq p
for some
b\in\cancel{\mathcal{D}}[U]
, i.e.
c\cancel{\mathcal{D}}b
for some
c\in U
(for which
c\leq o
). The proof of 4 is similar.
∎

5
Modal characterization of classes of relational algebras

Proposition 5.1
.

For any proto-subordination algebra
\mathbb{S}=(A,\prec)
,

1.

If
\mathbb{S}
is monotone, then

(i)

\mathbb{S}\models\;\prec\ \subseteq\ \leq\
iff
\ \mathbb{S}^{*}\models a\leq\Diamond a\
iff
\ \mathbb{S}^{*}\models\blacksquare a\leq a
.

(ii)

\mathbb{S}\models\;\leq\ \subseteq\ \prec\
iff
\ \mathbb{S}^{*}\models\Diamond a\leq a\
iff
\ \mathbb{S}^{*}\models a\leq\blacksquare a
.

(iii)

\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(T)}\quad
iff
\quad\mathbb{S}^{*}\models\Diamond a\leq\Diamond\Diamond a
.

(iv)

\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(D)}\quad
iff
\quad\mathbb{S}^{*}\models\Diamond\Diamond a\leq\Diamond a
.

2.

If
\mathbb{S}
is monotone and
\wedge
-SL based, then

(i)

\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(CT)}\
iff
\ \mathbb{S}^{*}\models\Diamond a\leq\Diamond(a\wedge\Diamond a)
.

(ii)

\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(SL2)}\
iff
\ \mathbb{S}^{*}\models\Diamond(\Diamond a\wedge\Diamond b)\leq\Diamond(a\wedge b)
.

3.

If
\mathbb{S}
is monotone and
\vee
-SL based, then

(i)

\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(SL1)}\
iff
\ \mathbb{S}^{*}\models\blacksquare(a\vee b)\leq\blacksquare(\blacksquare a\vee\blacksquare b)
.

(ii)

\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(S9\Leftarrow)}\
iff
\ \mathbb{S^{*}}\models\blacksquare a\vee\blacksquare b\leq\blacksquare(a\vee\blacksquare b)
.

(iii)

\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(S9\Rightarrow)}\
iff
\ \mathbb{S^{*}}\models\blacksquare(a\vee\blacksquare b)\leq\blacksquare a\vee\blacksquare b
.

(iv)

\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(DCT)}
iff
\ \mathbb{S^{*}}\models\blacksquare(a\vee\blacksquare a)\leq\blacksquare a
.

4.

if
\mathbb{S}
is monotone and based on
(A,\neg)
with
A\models\forall a\forall b(\neg a\leq b\Leftrightarrow\neg b\leq a)
,

(i)

\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(S6)}\
iff
\ \mathbb{S^{*}}\models\neg\Diamond a=\blacksquare\neg a
. Thus, if
A\models\neg\neg a=a
, then
\blacksquare a\coloneqq\neg\Diamond\neg a
.

5.

if
\mathbb{S}
is monotone and based on
(A,\neg)
with
A\models\forall a\forall b(a\leq\neg b\Leftrightarrow b\leq\neg a)
,

(i)

\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(S6)}\
iff
\ \mathbb{S^{*}}\models\Diamond\neg a=\neg\blacksquare a
. Thus, if
A\models\neg\neg a=a
, then
\Diamond a\coloneqq\neg\blacksquare\neg a
.

Proof.

In what follows, variables
a,b,c\ldots
range in
A
. Also, for the sake of a more concise and readable presentation, we will write conjunctions of relational atoms as chains; for instance,
a\leq b\ \&\ b\prec c
will be written as
a\leq b\prec c
.

1 (i)

\forall a(a\leq\Diamond a)

iff

\forall a\forall b(\Diamond a\leq b\Rightarrow a\leq b)

Proposition
2.6
.1(i)

iff

\forall a\forall b(a\prec b\Rightarrow a\leq b)

iff

\prec\ \subseteq\ \leq

(ii)

\forall a(\Diamond a\leq a)

iff

\forall a\forall b(a\leq b\Rightarrow\Diamond a\leq b)

Proposition
2.6
.1(i)

iff

Remark 5.2
.

By Proposition
5.1
1(i) and 2(i),
for any monotone proto-subordination algebra
\mathbb{S}
which is
\wedge
-SL based,
\mathbb{S}\models\prec\ \subseteq\ \leq
iff

\mathbb{S}^{\ast}\models a\leq\Diamond a
, which implies
\mathbb{S}^{\ast}\models\Diamond a\leq\Diamond(a\wedge\Diamond a)
, i.e.
\mathbb{S}\models\mathrm{(CT)}
. This observation generalizes

[
3
, Observation 15]
.

Proposition 5.3
.

For any proto-precontact algebra
\mathbb{C}=(A,\mathcal{C})
,

1.

if
\mathbb{C}
is antitone, then

(i)

\mathbb{C}\models\;\cancel{\mathcal{C}}\ \subseteq\ \leq\
iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models{\rhd}a\leq a
.

(ii)

\mathbb{C}\models\;\leq\ \subseteq\ \cancel{\mathcal{C}}\
iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models a\leq{\rhd}a
.

(iii)

\mathbb{C}\models\;\cancel{\mathcal{C}}\ \subseteq\ \geq\
iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models{\blacktriangleright}a\leq a
.

(iv)

\mathbb{C}\models\;\geq\ \subseteq\ \cancel{\mathcal{C}}\
iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models a\leq{\blacktriangleright}a
.

(v)

\mathbb{C}\models\;\mathrm{(NS)}
iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models a\wedge{\rhd}a\leq\bot
.

(vi)

\mathbb{C}\models\;\mathrm{(SFN)}
iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models\top\leq a\vee{\rhd}a
.

2.

if
\mathbb{C}
is a precontact algebra, then

(i)

\mathbb{C}\models\;\cancel{\mathcal{C}}^{-1}\circ\cancel{\mathcal{C}}\subseteq\cancel{\mathcal{C}}\
iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models{\rhd}a\leq{\rhd}{\rhd}a\
iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models{\rhd}a\leq{\blacktriangleright}{\rhd}a\
iff

\ \mathbb{C}\models\;\cancel{\mathcal{C}}^{-1}\circ\cancel{\mathcal{C}}\subseteq\cancel{\mathcal{C}}^{-1}

(ii)

\mathbb{C}\models\;\cancel{\mathcal{C}}^{-1}\circ\cancel{\mathcal{C}}\subseteq\cancel{\mathcal{C}}\
iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models{\blacktriangleright}a\leq{\blacktriangleright}{\blacktriangleright}a\
iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models{\blacktriangleright}a\leq{\rhd}{\blacktriangleright}a\
iff
\ \mathbb{C}\models\;\cancel{\mathcal{C}}^{-1}\circ\cancel{\mathcal{C}}\subseteq\cancel{\mathcal{C}}^{-1}

(iii)

\mathbb{C}\models\;\cancel{\mathcal{C}}\circ\cancel{\mathcal{C}}\subseteq\cancel{\mathcal{C}}\
iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models{\blacktriangleright}a\leq{\blacktriangleright}{\rhd}a\
iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models{\rhd}a\leq{\rhd}{\blacktriangleright}a
iff
\cancel{\mathcal{C}}^{-1}\circ\cancel{\mathcal{C}}^{-1}\subseteq\cancel{\mathcal{C}}^{-1}

(iv)

\mathbb{C}\models\;\cancel{\mathcal{C}}\circ\cancel{\mathcal{C}}\subseteq\cancel{\mathcal{C}}^{-1}
iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models{\rhd}a\leq{\blacktriangleright}{\blacktriangleright}a\
iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models{\blacktriangleright}a\leq{\rhd}{\rhd}a
iff
\cancel{\mathcal{C}}^{-1}\circ\cancel{\mathcal{C}}^{-1}\subseteq\cancel{\mathcal{C}}

3.

if
\mathbb{C}
is a lattice-based precontact algebra, then

(i)

\mathbb{C}\models\ \cancel{\mathcal{C}}\subseteq\ \cancel{\mathcal{C}}^{-1}\
iff
\ \mathbb{C}\models\ \cancel{\mathcal{C}}^{-1}\subseteq\ \cancel{\mathcal{C}}\
iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models{\rhd}a\leq{\blacktriangleright}a\
iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models a\leq{\rhd}{\rhd}a\
iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models{\blacktriangleright}a\leq{\rhd}a\
iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models a\leq{\blacktriangleright}{\blacktriangleright}a
.

4.

if
\mathbb{C}
is a
\wedge
-SL based precontact algebra, then

(i)

\mathbb{C}\models\;\mathrm{(CMO)}
iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models{\rhd}a\leq{\rhd}(a\wedge{\rhd}a)
.

5.

if
\mathbb{C}

iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models\top\leq{\rhd}a\vee{\rhd}{\rhd}a
.

(ii)

\mathbb{C}\models\;\mathrm{(ALT)}^{\blacktriangleright}
iff
\ \mathbb{C}^{*}\models\top\leq{\blacktriangleright}a\vee{\blacktriangleright}{\blacktriangleright}a

5. The proofs of this item make use of the fact that the precontact algebra

Proposition 5.4
.

For any dual proto-precontact algebra
\mathbb{D}=(A,\mathcal{D})
,

1.

\ \mathbb{D}^{*}\models{\blacktriangleleft}a\leq a
.

\ \mathbb{D}\models\;\cancel{\mathcal{D}}^{-1}\circ\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\subseteq\cancel{\mathcal{D}}^{-1}

\ \mathbb{D}^{*}\models{\blacktriangleleft}{\lhd}a\leq{\blacktriangleleft}a

\ \mathbb{D}^{*}\models{\lhd}{\lhd}a\leq{\blacktriangleleft}a\

\ \mathbb{D}^{*}\models{\blacktriangleleft}{\blacktriangleleft}a\leq{\lhd}a

.

We conclude this section by showing that the modal characterization results above naturally extend to relational algebras endowed with more than one relation. Two such examples arise when considering the interaction between obligation and permission in input/output logic and also in the context of the study of subordination and precontact algebras.

A
bi-subordination algebra

[
33
]
is a structure
\mathbb{B}=(A,\prec_{1},\prec_{2})
such that
(A,\prec_{i})
is a subordination algebra for
1\leq i\leq 2
. The following conditions on DL-based bi-subordination algebras have been considered in the literature (see also Section
6.4
for an expanded discussion on these conditions):

(P1)

c\prec_{1}a\vee b\Rightarrow\forall d[a\prec_{2}d\Rightarrow\exists e(e\prec_{1}b\ \&\ c\leq e\vee d)]

(P2)

a\wedge b\prec_{2}c\Rightarrow\forall d[d\prec_{1}a\Rightarrow\exists e(b\prec_{2}e\ \&\ d\wedge e\leq c)]
.

A
(proto-) subordination precontact algebra
, abbreviated as (proto) sp-algebra, is a structure
\mathbb{H}=(A,\prec,\mathcal{C})
such that
(A,\prec)
is a (proto-)subordination algebra, and
(A,\mathcal{C})
is a (proto-)precontact algebra
16
16

16

Likewise, we can define
(proto) sdp-algebras
as structures
\mathbb{K}=(A,\prec,\mathcal{D})
such that
(A,\prec)
is a (proto-)subordination algebra, and
(A,\mathcal{D})
is a dual (proto-)precontact algebra
. We will consider the following conditions on sp-algebras, some of which are the algebraic counterparts of well known conditions on the relationship between norms and permissions:
17
17

17

Conditions (CS1) and (CS2) crop up in the literature on subordination algebras as the defining conditions of pre-contact subordination lattices (cf.
[
13
, Definition 3.2.1]
). Interestingly, (CS2) is the algebraic counterpart of a closure rule known as (AND)
↓
in the input/output logic literature.

(CS1)

a\prec x\vee y\ \&\ a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}x\Rightarrow a\prec y

(CS2)

a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}x\wedge y\ \&\ a\prec x\Rightarrow a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}y
.

(CT)
↓

a\prec b\ \&\ a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}b\wedge c\Rightarrow a\wedge b\cancel{\mathcal{C}}c

(CT)
⊳

a\prec b\ \&\ a\wedge b\cancel{\mathcal{C}}c\Rightarrow a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}c
.

(INC)

a\prec b\Rightarrow a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}b

Slanted algebras of the appropriate modal similarity type can be associated both with bi-subordination algebras and with sp-algebras in the obvious way. The modal axioms characterizing conditions (P1) and (P2) in item 3 of the next proposition have been used in
[
34
]
to completely axiomatize the positive (i.e. negation-free) fragment of basic classical normal modal logic.

Proposition 5.5
.

For any bi-subordination algebra
\mathbb{B}=(A,\prec_{1},\prec_{2})
,

1.

\mathbb{B}\models\prec_{1}\ \subseteq\prec_{2}
iff

\mathbb{B}^{\ast}\models\Diamond_{2}a\leq\Diamond_{1}a
.

2.

\mathbb{B}\models\prec_{2}\circ\prec_{1}\ \subseteq\ \leq
iff
\mathbb{B}^{\ast}\models\blacksquare_{2}a\leq\Diamond_{1}a
.

3.

if
\mathbb{B}
is DL-based, then

(i)

\mathbb{B}\models\mathrm{(P1)}
iff
\mathbb{B}^{\ast}\models\blacksquare_{1}(a\vee b)\leq\Diamond_{2}a\vee\blacksquare_{1}b
.

(ii)

\mathbb{B}\models\mathrm{(P2)}
iff
\mathbb{B}^{\ast}\models\blacksquare_{1}a\wedge\Diamond_{2}b\leq\Diamond_{2}(a\wedge b)
.

4.

\mathbb{B}\models\prec_{2}\circ\prec_{1}\ \subseteq\ \prec_{1}\circ\prec_{2}
iff
\mathbb{B}^{\ast}\models\Diamond_{1}\blacksquare_{2}a\leq\blacksquare_{2}\Diamond_{1}a
.

5.

\mathbb{B}\models\prec_{1}\circ\prec_{2}\ \subseteq\ \prec_{1}
iff
\mathbb{B}^{\ast}\models\Diamond_{1}a\leq\Diamond_{2}\Diamond_{1}a
.

6.

\mathbb{B}\models\forall a\forall b(a\prec_{1}b\ \&\ a\prec_{2}b\Rightarrow b=\top)
iff
\mathbb{B}^{\ast}\models\Diamond_{1}a\vee\Diamond_{2}a=\top
.

7.

\mathbb{B}\models\forall b(\forall a(a\prec_{1}b)\Rightarrow\forall a(a\prec_{2}b))
iff
\mathbb{B}^{\ast}\models\Diamond_{2}\top\leq\Diamond_{1}\top
.

8.

\mathbb{B}\models\forall a(\exists b(a\prec_{1}b\ \&\ b\prec_{2}\bot)\Rightarrow a=\bot)
iff
\mathbb{B}^{\ast}\models\blacksquare_{1}\blacksquare_{2}\bot=\bot
.

Proof.

iff

Proposition 5.6
.

For any sp-algebra
\mathbb{H}=(A,\prec,\mathcal{C})
and any sdp-algebra
\mathbb{K}=(A,\prec,\mathcal{D})
,

1.

\mathbb{H}\models\mathrm{(INC)}
iff

\mathbb{H}^{\ast}\models\blacksquare a\leq{\rhd}a
.

2.

if
\mathbb{H}
is
\wedge
-SL-based, then

(i)

\mathbb{H}\models\mathrm{(CT)}^{\rhd}
iff

\mathbb{H}^{\ast}\models{\rhd}(a\wedge\Diamond a)\leq{\rhd}a
.

3.

if
\mathbb{H}
is Heyting algebra-based, then

(i)

\mathbb{H}\models\mathrm{(CS2)}
iff
\mathbb{H}^{\ast}\models\Diamond a\rightarrow{\rhd}a\leq{\rhd}a

(ii)

\mathbb{H}\models\mathrm{(CT)}^{\downarrow}
iff
\mathbb{H}^{\ast}\models\blacksquare b\wedge{\blacktriangleright}(b\wedge c)\leq b\rightarrow{\blacktriangleright}c
.

4.

if
\mathbb{H}
is co-Heyting algebra-based
18
18

18

A
co-Heyting algebra
is a distributive lattice
A
endowed with a binary operation

\,>\mkern-8.0mu\raisebox{-0.27986pt}{\rule[2.5252pt]{5.94167pt}{0.43057pt}}\,

s.t.
a\mbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\,>\mkern-8.0mu\raisebox{-0.27986pt}{\rule[2.5252pt]{5.94167pt}{0.43057pt}}\,$}}b\leq c
iff
a\leq c\vee b
for all
a,b,c\in A
.
, then

(i)

\mathbb{H}\models\mathrm{(CS1)}
iff
\mathbb{H}^{\ast}\models\Diamond a\leq\Diamond a\mbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\,>\mkern-8.0mu\raisebox{-0.27986pt}{\rule[2.5252pt]{5.94167pt}{0.43057pt}}\,$}}{\rhd}a
.

5.

if
\mathbb{K}
is co-Heyting algebra-based, then

(i)

\mathbb{K}\models\mathrm{(CT)^{\lhd}}
iff
\mathbb{K}^{\ast}\models{\lhd}a\leq\Diamond({\lhd}a\mbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\,>\mkern-8.0mu\raisebox{-0.27986pt}{\rule[2.5252pt]{5.94167pt}{0.43057pt}}\,$}}a)
.

Proof.

1.

\forall a(\blacksquare a\leq{\blacktriangleright}a)

iff

\forall a\forall b(b\leq\blacksquare a\Rightarrow b\leq{\blacktriangleright}a)

iff

\forall a\forall b(b\prec a\Rightarrow b\cancel{\mathcal{C}}a)

2(i)

\forall a({\rhd}(a\wedge\Diamond a)\leq{\rhd}a)

iff

\forall a\forall c(c\leq{\rhd}(a\wedge\Diamond a)\Rightarrow c\leq{\rhd}a)

iff

\forall a\forall c(\exists b(\Diamond a\leq b\ \&\ c\leq{\rhd}(a\wedge b))\Rightarrow c\leq{\rhd}a)

iff

\forall a\forall b\forall c(a\prec b\ \&\ (a\wedge b)\cancel{\mathcal{C}}c\Rightarrow a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}c)

3(i)

\forall a(\Diamond a\rightarrow{\rhd}a\leq{\rhd}a)

iff

\forall a\forall c(c\leq\Diamond a\rightarrow{\rhd}a\Rightarrow c\leq{\rhd}a)

iff

\forall a\forall c(\Diamond a\wedge c\leq{\rhd}a\Rightarrow c\leq{\rhd}a)

iff

\forall a\forall c(\exists b(\Diamond a\leq b\ \&\ b\wedge c\leq{\rhd}a)\Rightarrow c\leq{\rhd}a)

iff

\forall a\forall b\forall c(\Diamond a\leq b\ \&\ b\wedge c\leq{\rhd}a\Rightarrow c\leq{\rhd}a)

iff

\forall a\forall b\forall c(a\prec b\ \&\ a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}b\wedge c\Rightarrow a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}c)

(ii)

\forall b\forall c(\blacksquare b\wedge{\blacktriangleright}(b\wedge c)\leq b\rightarrow{\blacktriangleright}c)

iff

\forall a\forall b\forall c(a\leq\blacksquare b\wedge{\blacktriangleright}(b\wedge c)\Rightarrow a\leq b\rightarrow{\blacktriangleright}c)

iff

\forall a\forall b\forall c(a\leq\blacksquare b\ \&\ a\leq{\blacktriangleright}(b\wedge c)\Rightarrow(a\wedge b)\leq{\blacktriangleright}c)

iff

\forall a\forall b\forall c(a\prec b\ \&\ a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}(b\wedge c)\Rightarrow(a\wedge b)\cancel{\mathcal{C}}c)

4(i)

\forall a(\Diamond a\leq\Diamond a\mbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\,>\mkern-8.0mu\raisebox{-0.27986pt}{\rule[2.5252pt]{5.94167pt}{0.43057pt}}\,$}}{\rhd}a)

iff

\forall a\forall c(\Diamond a\mbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\,>\mkern-8.0mu\raisebox{-0.27986pt}{\rule[2.5252pt]{5.94167pt}{0.43057pt}}\,$}}{\rhd}a\leq c\Rightarrow\Diamond a\leq c)

iff

\forall a\forall c(\Diamond a\leq c\vee{\rhd}a\Rightarrow\Diamond a\leq c)

iff

\forall a\forall c(\exists b(b\leq{\rhd}a\ \&\ \Diamond a\leq c\vee b)\Rightarrow\Diamond a\leq c)

iff

\forall a\forall b\forall c(b\leq{\rhd}a\ \&\ \Diamond a\leq c\vee b\Rightarrow\Diamond a\leq c)

iff

\forall a\forall b\forall c(a\cancel{\mathcal{C}}b\ \&\ a\prec b\vee c\Rightarrow a\prec c)

5(i)

\forall a({\lhd}a\leq\Diamond({\lhd}a\mbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\,>\mkern-8.0mu\raisebox{-0.27986pt}{\rule[2.5252pt]{5.94167pt}{0.43057pt}}\,$}}a)

iff

\forall a\forall c(\Diamond({\lhd}a\mbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\,>\mkern-8.0mu\raisebox{-0.27986pt}{\rule[2.5252pt]{5.94167pt}{0.43057pt}}\,$}}a)\leq c\Rightarrow{\lhd}a\leq c)

iff

\forall a\forall c(\exists b(\Diamond(b\mbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\,>\mkern-8.0mu\raisebox{-0.27986pt}{\rule[2.5252pt]{5.94167pt}{0.43057pt}}\,$}}a)\leq c\ \&\ {\lhd}a\leq b)\Rightarrow{\lhd}a\leq c)

iff

\forall a\forall b\forall c(\Diamond(b\mbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\,>\mkern-8.0mu\raisebox{-0.27986pt}{\rule[2.5252pt]{5.94167pt}{0.43057pt}}\,$}}a)\leq c\ \&\ {\lhd}a\leq b\Rightarrow{\lhd}a\leq c)

iff

\forall a\forall b\forall c((b\mbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\,>\mkern-8.0mu\raisebox{-0.27986pt}{\rule[2.5252pt]{5.94167pt}{0.43057pt}}\,$}}a)\prec c\ \&\ a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}b\Rightarrow a\cancel{\mathcal{D}}c)

∎

6
Applications

In the present section, we discuss four independent but connected applications of the characterization results of the previous section.

6.1
Characterizing output operators

The output operators
out^{N}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq 6
associated with a given input/output logic
\mathbb{L}=(\mathcal{L},N)
can be given semantic counterparts in the environment of proto-subordination algebras as follows: for every proto-subordination algebra
\mathbb{S}=(A,\prec)
, we let
\mathbb{S}_{i}:=(A,\prec_{i})
where
{\prec_{i}}\subseteq A\times A
is the smallest extension of
\prec
which satisfies the properties indicated in the following table:
19
19

19

While (the purely logical counterparts of) outputs 1 to 4 are well known in the literature of input/output logic (cf.
[
3
]
), the last two outputs are novel.

\prec_{i}

Properties

\prec_{1}

(
\top
), (SI), (WO), (AND)

\prec_{2}

(
\top
), (SI), (WO), (AND), (OR)

\prec_{3}

Proposition 6.1
.

For any defined proto-subordination algebra
\mathbb{S}=(A,\prec)
,

1.

\Diamond_{1}
is the largest monotone map dominated by
\Diamond
(i.e. pointwise-smaller than or equal to

).

2.

\Diamond_{2}
is the largest regular map dominated by
\Diamond
.

3.

\Diamond_{3}
is the largest monotone map satisfying
\Diamond_{3}a\leq\Diamond_{3}(a\wedge\Diamond_{3}a)
dominated by
\Diamond
.

4.

\Diamond_{4}
is the largest regular map satisfying
\Diamond_{4}a\leq\Diamond_{4}(a\wedge\Diamond_{4}a)
dominated by
\Diamond
.

5.

\Diamond_{5}
is the largest normal map dominated by
\Diamond
.

6.

\Diamond_{6}
is the largest normal map dominated by
\Diamond
, the adjoint of which satisfies
\blacksquare_{6}(a\vee\blacksquare_{6}a)\leq\blacksquare_{6}a
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.5
and Proposition
5.1
, the properties stated in each item of the statement hold for
\Diamond_{i}
and
\blacksquare_{i}
. To complete the proof, we need to argue for
\Diamond_{i}
being the largest such map By Lemma
4.2
1.(ii),
a\prec_{i}b
iff
\Diamond_{i}a\leq b
for all
a,b\in A
and
1\leq i\leq 4
. Any
f:A\to A^{\delta}
s.t.
f(a)\in K(A^{\delta})
for every
a\in A
induces a proto-subordination relation
\prec_{f}\subseteq A\times A
defined as
a\prec_{f}b
iff
f(a)\leq b
. Clearly, if
f(a)\leq f^{\prime}(a)
for every
a\in A

{\prec}_{f^{\prime}}\subseteq{\prec_{f}}
. Moreover, if

for some

s.t.

.

If
\Diamond_{i}
is not the largest map endowed with the properties mentioned in the statement and dominated by
\Diamond
, then a map
f

for all

for some

. As
f

\prec_{f}
is an extension of
\prec
which enjoys the required properties, and is strictly contained in
\prec_{i}
. Hence,
\prec_{i}
is not the smallest such extension.
∎
